🎯 Best Ideas— undervalued & high-conviction now: 6 deep memos + 9 consensus picks

The week's highest-conviction names, ranked by cross-specialist consensus. Top picks carry a full Opus research memo (business, bull & bear, valuation, catalysts, what to watch); the rest carry the specialist thesis. MoS = margin of safety vs DCF. Click any card to expand.

#1DLODLocal LimitedBUY — high-quality, net-cash EMEDIUMCOREMoS +85%PEG 0.79▼

Endorsed by: 🌀 Sleep Scale Economics Shared + 🔄 Marks Second-Level + 👁️ Insider & 13F Tracker + 🕳️ Burry Catalyst Deep Value

Business: DLocal runs a cross-border payment platform connecting large global merchants (Netflix, Amazon, Spotify, Uber) to consumers across 40+ emerging markets in LatAm, Africa and Asia, handling local payment methods that incumbent global processors can't natively support. It earns a take rate on total payment volume (TPV) processed. Its moat is payment fragmentation: managing hundreds of local rails, currencies, settlement relationships and regulatory regimes through one API is genuinely hard to replicate, and scale in a given market improves FX rates, settlement cost and bank relationships — a real Sleep-Scale dynamic.

Bull case: DLocal is a rare combination of high growth, capital efficiency and a clean balance sheet. FY25 revenue hit $1.09B (+47% YoY) with operating income of $222M (~20% operating margin), net income of $197M, and it sits on ~$424M cash against just $90M debt for ~$334M net cash — it self-funds its expansion. Q1'26 momentum is even stronger: TPV grew 73% YoY, evidence the volume flywheel is intact and that it is winning more wallet share inside its mega-merchant accounts. The SES thesis holds: as DLocal scales volume per market, unit economics improve and it competes on efficiency rather than rent extraction. With a long EM digitization runway, management guiding to disciplined opex (hiring freeze), and analysts modeling revenue toward ~$2.9–3.4B by 2029–2030, this can compound earnings for years. At ~19x trailing P/E for a business growing volumes 70%+ with 34% ROIC and double-digit FCF yield, the price is not demanding for the quality.

Bear case: The screener's headline numbers mask the core risk: take-rate compression. Q1'26 revenue grew only 55% while TPV grew 73% — meaning DLocal earns progressively less per dollar processed as large enterprise clients use their leverage to negotiate fees down. Gross margin has slid from ~43% (2022) to ~37% (FY25), and operating income in FY25 ($222M) is barely above 2023 ($180M) despite revenue nearly doubling — incremental dollars are far less profitable than the past. This is the central tension of the SES story: efficiency that gets passed to customers can become commoditization. Add concentrated merchant dependence (loss of one whale hurts), heavy EM FX and political/regulatory risk across dozens of jurisdictions, a history of short-seller scrutiny, and the structural ceiling that take rates only go one direction with scale. The DCF intrinsic value of $81.71 is not credible — it implies ~7x upside that no analyst endorses.

Valuation: Genuinely cheap on growth-adjusted metrics but the screener overstates it. The DCF margin-of-safety of +86% (fair value $81.71) is an outlier I would discard — it's wildly disconnected from the analyst consensus price target of $17 (~48% upside from $11.52). Trailing P/E ~19x with TPV growth of 70%+ gives a flattering PEG well under 1, and an ~11% FCF yield on a net-cash balance sheet is attractive. But you must haircut for take-rate decay. My rough fair-value view sits in the $15–18 range — broadly the analyst consensus — implying meaningful but not extraordinary upside; treat anything above $20 as requiring take-rate stabilization.

Catalysts: Q2 2026 earnings on August 13, 2026 (post-market) is the key near-term event — the market will scrutinize whether net take rate stabilizes and whether the hiring freeze drives operating leverage. New large-merchant wins or geographic expansion announcements, continued 60%+ TPV growth, and any upward estimate revisions could re-rate the stock toward the $17 target. Recovering sentiment in LatAm fintech (the MercadoLibre selloff dragged the cohort) is a possible sector tailwind.

What to watch (4–12 wks): 1) Net take rate trajectory — the single most important metric; stabilization would be very bullish, further decline confirms the bear case. 2) The Aug 13 Q2 print and guidance. 3) Operating margin recovery as the hiring freeze flows through. 4) Insider activity — note the recent open-market purchase of 20,000 shares at $11.85 by William Rodney Pruett (a mild positive signal). 5) TPV vs revenue growth spread narrowing or widening. 6) EM FX moves and any regulatory headlines across key markets.

Time horizon: 2-3 years — this is a growth compounder thesis that needs time for scale economics to outrun take-rate compression, anchored by the Aug 2026 earnings catalyst.

#2DUOLDuolingo, Inc.WATCH — a genuinely high-qualiMEDIUMSATELLITEMoS +74%PEG 0.66▼

Endorsed by: 🔄 Marks Second-Level + 🚀 Wood Disruptive Innovation

Business: Duolingo runs the world's most-downloaded language-learning app, monetizing a large free user base through Super/Max subscriptions (the bulk of revenue) and in-app advertising, with newer expansion into math and music. The model is freemium-to-subscription at scale, generating ~72% gross margins, strong free cash flow and a net-cash balance sheet (~$1.04B cash vs ~$94M debt). Its competitive moat is brand dominance in consumer language learning, a gamified habit loop driving high DAU/MAU stickiness, and an AI-driven content/teaching layer (Max) that few peers can match.

Bull case: This is a rare combination of durable ~39% revenue growth (FY24 $748M → FY25 $1,038M) with rapidly expanding profitability: operating income roughly doubled from $64M to $141M, and the business throws off real free cash flow on a fortress balance sheet (~$943M net cash). The drawdown of 50%+ from the highs looks driven primarily by multiple compression in growth tech rather than fundamental deterioration — MAU/DAU and paid-subscriber growth remain double-digit at scale, and the AI 'Max' tier offers a credible per-user monetization ramp. Analyst forward estimates (2027 EPS ~$3.40, 2028 ~$3.91; 2027 revenue ~$1.37B, 2028 ~$1.54B) imply continued strong compounding, and if Duolingo sustains 25-35% growth with widening margins, today's price could prove cheap in 3-5 years. A net-cash, profitable, category-leading consumer platform that has been cut in half is exactly the kind of dislocation a patient compounding investor wants to own.

Bear case: The screener's deep-value framing is misleading: the TTM net income of $414M massively exceeds operating income of $141M because it includes a large one-time, non-cash tax benefit (likely a deferred-tax-asset valuation release). Strip that out and DUOL trades at roughly 30-35x forward EPS and ~8x forward sales — a premium growth multiple, not the 11.8x P/E or $456 DCF the screen implies (the +74% margin of safety is not real). The credible bears: growth is decelerating (forward estimates imply rev growth slowing toward ~30% then ~12%), consumer subscription churn is sensitive to engagement fatigue, and generative-AI tutors (ChatGPT, Gemini) could commoditize language tutoring and compress Duolingo's differentiation. Consensus price target is just $148 (median $120) — only modest upside — meaning the Street already sees fair-ish value, not a steal. Stock-based comp and a consumer-discretionary demand profile add further risk in a soft macro.

Valuation: NOT the deep-value setup the screener shows. The P/E of 11.8 and DCF of $456 are distorted by a one-time tax benefit inflating net income to $414M; on operating earnings and forward analyst EPS (~$3.40 for 2027, ~$3.91 for 2028) the stock trades around 30-35x forward earnings and ~8x forward sales — a premium growth valuation. PEG of 0.3 is not real once you normalize earnings. Against consensus PT of $148 (median $120, range $95-$245), the stock at $119 sits right at median fair value with ~25% upside to mean target. Fair value is roughly $120-150; reasonably valued for the growth, neither cheap nor egregious.

Catalysts: Next quarterly earnings (paid-subscriber net adds, Max attach rate, bookings guidance) is the key re-rater; continued risk-on rotation into beaten-down growth software (the +8-10% move on 2026-06-08 shows sensitivity); evidence that AI 'Max' is lifting ARPU/monetization; positive estimate revisions; and any sign of re-accelerating MAU/DAU growth.

What to watch (4–12 wks): The upcoming Q2 earnings date and specifically: paid-subscriber and DAU/MAU growth rates (watch for deceleration vs the ~39% revenue trend), Max attach and ARPU, and bookings guidance; whether 2027/2028 EPS estimates revise up or down; the durability of the recent sentiment-driven bounce; and gross-margin trend (currently ~72%). Insider activity so far is only routine director stock awards — no signal. Also monitor competitive-AI headlines that could pressure the narrative.

Time horizon: 3-5 years for the compounding thesis to play out; near-term it is a WATCH pending a better entry or confirmation that growth/margins are holding — add on pullbacks or a strong subscriber print rather than chasing the bounce.

#3QLYSQualys, Inc.WATCH — exceptionally capital-MEDIUMSATELLITEMoS +64%PEG 1.82▼

Endorsed by: 🔄 Marks Second-Level + 👁️ Insider & 13F Tracker

Business: Qualys sells cloud-delivered cybersecurity and compliance software (VMDR — vulnerability management, detection and response) as recurring SaaS subscriptions to large enterprises and regulated/government clients. It earns high-margin, sticky revenue from security teams continuously scanning and remediating vulnerabilities across complex IT estates. It is a profitable mid-cap incumbent (FY25 revenue $669M, 83% gross margin, 33% operating margin, ~$198M net income) competing against larger, better-funded players like Tenable, Rapid7, CrowdStrike and Wiz in a consolidating market.

Bull case: This is a genuinely elite-quality business hiding behind a pedestrian multiple. FY25 revenue grew ~10% to $669M with operating margin of 33% (up from 21% in FY21), net income of $198M, and a fortress balance sheet ($250M cash, $153M net cash, minimal debt). At 17.6x trailing earnings with an ~8% FCF yield, an investor is paying a value multiple for a software franchise generating ~29% ROIC and consistent double-digit growth — a rare combination. Margins have expanded every year while the company self-funds aggressive buybacks, so per-share metrics compound faster than the topline. If Qualys can sustain ~10% growth and continue widening margins toward 35%+, EPS marches from today's ~$6 toward the $8.7–$10 analysts model for 2028–2029, and even a modest re-rating toward a software-average multiple delivers strong returns. It is the kind of durable, cash-generative compounder that quietly outperforms.

Bear case: The headline 'second-level' setup is partly stale: the stock is up ~23% since the last print and ~9% in a single late-May session, and at $108 it trades ABOVE the analyst median price target of $100 (consensus $103) — so the screener's '+65% margin of safety vs $305 DCF' is an outlier that the sell-side flatly disagrees with, and the easy disconnect has largely closed. Growth is decelerating (25%→19%→12%→10% over four years) in a market where faster, deeper-pocketed rivals (CrowdStrike, Wiz, Microsoft) are bundling exposure-management features that could commoditize Qualys's core. There is no insider cluster buying to support the 'Insider Tracker' tag — recent activity is small routine CEO/officer sales. A 10%-grower at 17–18x is reasonably, not cheaply, priced once you discount the aggressive DCF; multiple compression or a single growth disappointment could quickly erase the recent gains.

Valuation: Fairly valued to modestly undervalued — not the 65% bargain the DCF implies. At $108 / 17.6x TTM P/E with an ~8% FCF yield, QLYS is cheap for its quality but rich relative to its ~10% growth (PEG ~1.1). The $305 DCF is an aggressive outlier; the more credible market view is the analyst consensus of $100–103 (median $100, high $125), meaning the stock currently sits slightly above fair value on the Street's math. My rough fair-value band is ~$105–125 — fair today, with real upside only if growth reaccelerates or margins keep expanding. The bull/bear gap hinges entirely on whether you trust the DCF (~$305) or the consensus (~$100).

Catalysts: Next quarterly earnings (Qualys reports late July/early August) with billings/net-revenue-retention and FY guidance; continued operating-margin expansion above ~33%; new-product traction (TotalCloud/CyberSecurity Asset Management) and large government/regulated contract wins; sector M&A — as a profitable ~$3.8B net-cash franchise, Qualys is a plausible acquisition target amid security consolidation; renewed/expanded buyback authorization.

What to watch (4–12 wks): The Q2 earnings date and report (watch revenue growth holding ~10%+, net revenue retention, and operating margin vs the 33% run-rate); whether the thin analyst coverage (4–9 estimates) revises 2027–2028 numbers up after the recent rally; any shift from routine insider selling to actual buying (none seen yet); momentum follow-through after the +23% post-earnings move and the May 29 +8.9% pop; competitive headlines from Tenable/CrowdStrike/Wiz on exposure management.

Time horizon: 2–3 years — a quality-compounder hold, but better entered on a pullback toward the low-$90s/below consensus PT rather than chased after the recent run.

#4ADBEAdobe Inc.BUY — a high-quality compoundeMEDIUMWATCHMoS +37%PEG 1.54▼

Endorsed by: 🌀 Sleep Scale Economics Shared

Business: Adobe sells creative software (Photoshop, Premiere, Acrobat) and an enterprise Digital Experience suite (analytics, campaign management, personalisation) almost entirely on a recurring SaaS basis to designers, marketers and CMOs globally. The business is exceptionally high-quality: FY25 revenue of $23.8B at an 89% gross margin, a 37% operating margin ($8.7B op income), $7.1B net income and ~37% ROIC. Deep workflow lock-in across professionals and enterprises gives it durable pricing power, and it is layering generative AI (Firefly, GenStudio) on top of an installed base most rivals cannot easily reach.

Bull case: Adobe is the textbook 'scale economics shared' compounder: revenue grew from $15.8B (FY21) to $23.8B (FY25) — a ~11%+ CAGR — while gross margin held at 88-89% and operating margin expanded toward 37%, showing it reinvests scale gains rather than over-harvesting them. Operating income rose from $5.8B to $8.7B over the same span (+50%), net income from $4.8B to $7.1B. The balance sheet is pristine (net debt only $1.2B against $9.7B EBITDA, easily <0.2x). At a ~14x trailing P/E and ~10% FCF yield, the market is pricing terminal decline for a franchise still compounding double digits. If Firefly/GenStudio convert Adobe's distribution moat into AI monetisation — rather than the bear's cannibalisation — the stock can re-rate sharply toward consensus PT ($324.65) and the DCF ($351). Heavy buybacks (equity falling from $14.1B to $11.6B reflects aggressive repurchase) compound per-share value while the multiple is depressed.

Bear case: The core bear case is real and not yet disproven: generative-AI tools (OpenAI image/video, Midjourney, Google, Canva) threaten to commoditise exactly the creative work Adobe charges for, and the stock is down ~23% YTD precisely because investors fear Adobe's AI products defend ARPU rather than expand it. Revenue growth has decelerated to ~11% and the durability of pricing power is the open question. Insider signalling is unsupportive: CEO Narayen sold ~75k shares in late April near $243-245, and other insiders also sold — no cluster buying to mark a bottom. A cheap multiple can stay cheap (or get cheaper) if growth slips toward high single digits and the AI narrative hardens. The June 11 print is a binary near-term risk: guidance or net-new-ARR softness could trigger another leg down given elevated options-implied volatility.

Valuation: Cleanly undervalued on most lenses but not without reason. P/E ~14x TTM against ~11% growth and 37% ROIC is a discount to both the software group and Adobe's own history; the trailing PEG of ~0.41 and a ~10% FCF yield are unusually attractive for a franchise of this quality. The DCF implies $351 (+33% margin of safety) and sell-side consensus PT is $324.65 (~38% upside from $235.35), with a low-end PT of $220 capturing the bear scenario. A reasonable fair-value range is ~$300-340 if growth holds; the gap to price is essentially the market's discount for AI-disruption uncertainty. Asymmetry favors the buyer here.

Catalysts: Fiscal Q2 earnings after the close on June 11, 2026 is the dominant near-term catalyst — net-new Digital Media ARR, Firefly/GenStudio monetisation metrics and FY guidance can re-rate the stock either direction. Beyond that: evidence of AI products driving ARPU/seat expansion, continued buyback pace, and any positive estimate revisions (Stifel already raised its target into the print).

What to watch (4–12 wks): (1) June 11 Q2 results — focus on net-new ARR, Digital Media growth durability, and whether management quantifies AI monetisation rather than just engagement. (2) FY2026 guidance vs. the ~11% trajectory. (3) Estimate-revision direction post-print (2029/2030 EPS consensus sits at $30.88/$33.34). (4) Insider activity — watch for any shift from the recent net-selling toward buying. (5) Competitive headlines on GenAI creative tools eroding seat counts.

Time horizon: 2-3 years to let AI monetisation and multiple re-rating play out, with the June 11 earnings as a near-term volatility checkpoint that may offer a better entry.

#5ADMAADMA Biologics, Inc.WATCH — exceptional underlyingMEDIUMWATCHPEG 0.56▼

Endorsed by: 👁️ Insider & 13F Tracker

Business: ADMA is a vertically-integrated plasma-derived biologics company: it collects human plasma through its own donor centres, fractionates it at its own FDA-licensed facility, and sells finished immune-globulin therapies (chiefly BIVIGAM and the higher-margin specialty IVIG ASCENIV) to hospitals and specialty pharmacies, primarily for patients with primary immune deficiency. Owning the full chain — collection, manufacturing, and a differentiated specialty product — is its competitive edge in a supply-constrained plasma market dominated by far larger players like CSL, Grifols and Takeda. The economics have inflected sharply as ASCENIV mix and capacity utilisation improved.

Bull case: The fundamental story is one of the cleanest turnarounds in healthcare: revenue compounded from $81M (2021) to $258M (2023) to $426M (2024) to $510M (2025), gross profit margin expanded to ~57% and operating margin to ~37.5% ($191M operating income on $510M revenue), and the company swung from deep losses to $147M of net income while moving from net debt of ~$90M (2023) to a net cash position (-$7.7M net debt, $88M cash) by end-2025. Demand for Ig therapies is structurally supply-constrained and growing, and ASCENIV is a differentiated, premium-priced product with limited competition. Chairman Jerrold Grossman has been buying stock in the open market in May 2026 (multiple purchases around $7.91–$8.01), a constructive insider signal. If the legal overhang proves immaterial, a profitable, net-cash, ~20%+ grower at ~12x trailing earnings is cheap, and the pre-controversy analyst price target of $21 implies large upside.

Bear case: The dominant risk is not operational — it is disclosure integrity. In the first week of June 2026 multiple securities firms (Rosen, Pomerantz, Bleichmar Fonti & Auld) launched investigations explicitly alleging ADMA 'misrepresented its revenue sources,' and the stock was added to the Zacks Strong Sell list. When the bull thesis rests almost entirely on a steep, recent revenue/margin ramp and credible parties are questioning whether that revenue was accurately characterised, the entire model is in question until resolved. Additionally, the headline 'PE of 12x' is flattered: 2024 net income ($197M) exceeded operating income ($139M), indicating a large one-time deferred-tax-asset benefit, so normalised earnings power is lower than the trailing figure suggests. The business is also highly concentrated in two products and dependent on plasma supply and continued FDA compliance — a single manufacturing or regulatory setback would be severe given the lack of diversification.

Valuation: On reported trailing numbers ADMA screens cheap — ~12x P/E and a sub-1 PEG against ~20% growth — and on a clean DCF the net-cash balance sheet plus 37% operating margins would support a fair value comfortably above the current $8.12, consistent with the stale $21 consensus target. But that target predates the fraud allegations and the normalised (ex-tax-benefit) earnings base is lower than headline. Adjusting for tax-benefit-inflated earnings and applying a discount for legal/disclosure uncertainty, I view fair value as a wide range (~$8–$15) rather than a point estimate. The stock is plausibly undervalued IF the revenue allegations prove baseless; it is potentially overvalued if any restatement follows. That binary risk is why I refuse to call it cheap with confidence today.

Catalysts: (1) A company response, 8-K, or press statement directly addressing/refuting the revenue-misrepresentation allegations — the single most important catalyst. (2) The next quarterly 10-Q and earnings print, where investors will scrutinise revenue composition and any disclosure changes. (3) Resolution or dismissal of the law-firm investigations (or, negatively, the filing of a formal class action). (4) Continued open-market insider buying signalling management conviction. (5) ASCENIV volume/mix updates and any plasma-capacity expansion news.

What to watch (4–12 wks): Over the next 4–12 weeks: any 8-K or official rebuttal regarding the securities investigations; whether any of the law-firm probes escalate into a filed complaint; the upcoming 10-Q for revenue-recognition language and segment detail; reinstatement or continuation of the Zacks Strong Sell rank and any restored analyst coverage with fresh estimates; and whether Chairman Grossman (or other insiders) keep buying — additional cluster purchases would be reassuring, while insider selling would compound the concern.

Time horizon: 2-3 years for the fundamental story to play out, but the position should be gated on near-term (next 1-2 quarter) resolution of the disclosure allegations — do not size up until that overhang clears.

#6EXELExelixis, Inc.WATCH — genuinely high-qualityMEDIUMWATCHMoS +76%PEG 0.26ᵀ▼

Endorsed by: 👁️ Insider & 13F Tracker

Business: Exelixis is a profitable US oncology biotech whose economics revolve almost entirely around one franchise, cabozantinib (Cabometyx/Cometriq), approved across renal cell, hepatocellular and thyroid cancers. It earns money via direct US product sales plus royalties on Ipsen's ex-US sales, and is reinvesting cash flow into a next-gen pipeline (XL309 and others). Unlike most biotech, it is firmly cash-generative — FY2025 revenue of $2.32B at a 96% gross margin and ~38% operating margin — but its competitive moat is narrow and tied to the patent and clinical durability of a single molecule.

Bull case: This is a rare biotech that has crossed into durable profitability: FY2025 (period ending Jan 2026) delivered $2.32B revenue (+7% YoY), $872M operating income (37.6% margin), and $783M net income — up ~50% from the prior year's $521M — funded entirely from operations with a net-cash balance sheet (~$309M net cash, total debt only $173M). At ~15.5x trailing P/E and a ~7.3% FCF yield, an investor is paying a modest multiple for franchise-level margins (96% gross, 35% ROIC) that most healthcare names never reach. If the pipeline (XL309 and label-expansion trials for cabozantinib) produces even one new approved indication, the franchise life extends past the current patent overhang, and the stock could re-rate from a 'declining-asset' multiple toward a 'growing specialty pharma' multiple — a plausible double over multiple years. The buyback-and-cash-generation profile gives downside support even without that.

Bear case: The bear case is concentration and the looming revenue plateau. Nearly all profit comes from cabozantinib, and the sell-side does not believe the growth lasts: consensus revenue rises to ~$3.44B by 2030 but then *falls* to ~$2.98B by 2031, signalling that analysts are already pricing the patent/IP cliff and competitive erosion. Tellingly, the consensus 12-month price target is $47.33 — roughly 10% *below* the current $52.27 — and the high target is just $54, so professional analysts see little-to-no upside here, directly contradicting the screener's $219 DCF and 0.26 PEG. Those headline cheapness metrics are built on extrapolating trailing growth that the market expects to reverse, which is the classic signature of a value trap. Insider activity offers no offsetting comfort: recent Form 4s show small executive *sales* and routine stock awards, not the cluster buying that would validate a contrarian bull thesis.

Valuation: Mixed and contradictory depending on the lens. On trailing fundamentals it looks cheap — ~15.5x P/E, ~7.3% FCF yield, net cash — and the screener's $219 DCF implies +76% upside. But that DCF assumes growth persistence the Street rejects: forward estimates show revenue peaking near 2030 then declining, and analyst consensus price target of $47.33 implies ~10% downside, with the most bullish analyst at only $54 (~3% upside). The honest read is that EXEL is fairly-to-modestly-cheap as a stable cash machine but is NOT a 76%-undervalued compounder; a defensible fair value sits roughly in the high-$40s to high-$50s range — close to today's price — with optionality skewed to pipeline outcomes rather than the trailing-growth math.

Catalysts: Pipeline data readouts and label-expansion decisions for cabozantinib (new tumor types) and early XL309 development updates; further patent/IP litigation or settlement clarity that defines the post-cliff revenue tail; continued capital returns (buybacks) given net cash; and quarterly results that either confirm or break the +7% revenue trend. A positive trial readout that extends franchise duration is the single biggest re-rating lever.

What to watch (4–12 wks): Next quarterly earnings (last report was ~30 days ago per June news, so next print is ~late July/early Aug) — watch whether revenue growth holds near the 7% pace and whether margins stay near 38%; direction of analyst estimate revisions and any price-target moves above the current $54 ceiling; specific cabozantinib indication or XL309 trial milestone dates; and insider behavior — look for a shift from the recent small executive sales toward genuine open-market buying.

Time horizon: 2-3 years — long enough for a pipeline catalyst to either extend the franchise (bull) or for the patent-cliff overhang to crystallize (bear); not a 12-month catalyst trade given analysts currently see flat-to-negative near-term price upside.

Thesis: This is almost a textbook Magic Formula situation: a dominant, recession-resistant franchise with 39% ROIC and a 20% FCF margin trading at just 6.7x earnings and a PEG of 0.4. The market is pricing HRB as if tax filing is a dying business — but the financials tell a completely different story. Reven

🌀 Sleep Scale Economics Shared — 3 picks ▼
🌀 Sleep Scale Economics Shared
Nick Sleep's flywheel framework — businesses that share scale benefits with customers (Costco/Amazon model), driving self-reinforcing growth as they scale.
📖 Strategy details▼

Nick Sleep's Scale Economics Shared framework — the methodology that identified Amazon, Costco, and Berkshire decades early, based on companies that share their scale advantages with customers to build a self-reinforcing flywheel.

  • Core test: does the company get MORE efficient and offer BETTER prices as it grows? (opposite of most businesses)
  • Customer obsession: willing to take short-term margin hits to give customers more value (Costco 15% margin discipline)
  • Flywheel: lower prices → more customers → more scale → lower costs → even lower prices (self-reinforcing loop)
  • Long-term thinking evidence: quarterly earnings suppressed for multi-year investment; founder letters emphasise decades
  • Customer lifetime value: expanding over time as the business creates more value per customer
  • Non-obvious moat: the advantage isn't patents or brand — it's a culture and business model the organisation embodies
  • Capital allocation: cash flow reinvested into high-return opportunities rather than buybacks at peak prices
ADBE Adobe Inc. · Technology HIGH Stalwart Fast Grower Turnaround ▼
89% gross margin held steady across 4 years of rapid revenue scaling — proof the flywheel
🏢 About
Adobe sells creative and document software (Photoshop, Premiere, Acrobat) on a subscription basis to designers, marketers, and enterprises globally, and is expanding rapidly into AI-generated content via its Firefly platform and GenStudio marketing suite. Revenue is almost entirely recurring SaaS, with deep lock-in from professional workflows. Its Digital Experience segment serves enterprise CMOs with analytics, campaign management, and personalisation tools.
Industry: Creative & Marketing Software (SaaS) · vs: Canva, Figma (privately held), Microsoft Designer / Copilot

"This is the clearest Scale Economics Shared compounder in the entire candidate universe. Revenue has compounded from $15.8B to $23.8B over four years while gross margins have barely moved — sitting at 88-89% throughout — which is the textbook signal that Adobe is not pocketing scale efficiencies but reinvesting them into deeper product capability and lower friction for customers. The market is pricing in permanent AI disruption at a trailing PE of 14x and a forward PEG of 0.4, effectively treating Firefly as irrelevant. That is wrong: Adobe is the only player with both the IP rights infrastructure (licensed content training data), the professional distribution moat, and the enterprise sales motion to monetise generative AI at scale. Down 23% year-to-date and sitting at 56% of its 52-week high, the stock is pricing in a scenario the financials simply do not support. Earnings on June 11 is the immediate catalyst where management can reset the narrative."

The market is confusing AI as an Adobe disruptor when it is actually Adobe's most powerful product expansion in 20 years — and paying 14x earnings for the privilege of being wrong.

🔄 Marks Second-Level — 4 picks ▼
🔄 Marks Second-Level
Howard Marks' contrarian framework — find where market consensus is factually wrong. Oversold names with improving fundamentals; sentiment-extreme sectors with mean-reversion.
📖 Strategy details▼

Howard Marks' second-level thinking — first-level thinking gets average returns; second-level thinking ('the company is great BUT priced for perfection') finds the real opportunities.

  • Consensus audit: what is the mainstream view, and is it based on sound analysis or herd mentality?
  • Second-level question: what do I know or see that the consensus is missing?
  • Oversold opportunities: stocks down 50%+ where the negative narrative has become excessive vs fundamentals
  • Sentiment extremes: sectors at multi-year sentiment lows (rare to be wrong buying) or highs (rare to be right buying)
  • Narrative vs numbers gap: where does the market story ('this industry is dying') contradict the actual data?
  • Time horizon arbitrage: quarterly-focused Wall Street missing a multi-year thesis
  • Insider buying signal: executives aggressively buying when the market is selling — the strongest bullish insider indicator
DLO DLocal Limited · Technology HIGH Fast Grower Turnaround ▼
ROIC of 34% with revenue growing +47% YoY and a net cash position of $334M — a trifecta th
🏢 About
DLocal is a payments technology platform that connects global merchants — think Netflix, Amazon, and other multinationals — to consumers across emerging markets in Latin America, Africa, and Asia. It earns revenue on the spread between what merchants pay and what it disburses through local payment rails. Its customers are large global enterprises that need a single API to accept and send payments in dozens of currencies across fragmented local ecosystems.
Industry: Emerging Markets Payments Processing · vs: Adyen, Stripe (private), EBANX (private)

"The consensus has lumped DLO in with a broad 'fintech selloff' narrative and hasn't revisited it — but the actual financials tell a completely different story. Revenue compounded from $244M in 2021 to $1.09B in 2025, operating income hit $222M last year, net cash on the balance sheet swelled to $334M, and ROIC is running at 34%. This isn't a speculative fintech; it's a highly profitable infrastructure business in markets that are genuinely under-banked and growing structurally. The market is pricing a narrative (risky EM fintech) that the numbers flatly contradict. At PE=19 and PEG=0.4 with 47% revenue growth, you're buying a quality compounder at a distressed valuation. The 4.2% dividend yield from a net-cash balance sheet is the market's way of admitting it doesn't know what to do with this thing. The insider purchase at $11.85 in May 2026 is an executive putting real money where it counts."

The market is pricing DLO as a risky EM speculative fintech; it is actually a profitable infrastructure toll road with 34% ROIC, net cash, and 47% revenue growth — a classic narrative-vs-numbers gap.

🕳️ Burry Catalyst Deep Value — 4 picks ▼
🕳️ Burry Catalyst Deep Value
Michael Burry's catalyst-driven deep value. Hidden assets, sum-of-parts gaps, accounting normalisations — with a SPECIFIC named catalyst (≤12mo) that forces market repricing.
📖 Strategy details▼

Michael Burry's catalyst-driven deep value approach — finding mispriced assets where specific, identifiable catalysts will force the market to recognise hidden value within a defined timeframe.

  • Hidden assets: companies trading below the value of real estate, cash, or investments on the balance sheet
  • Sum-of-parts: conglomerates where individual divisions are worth more than the combined market cap
  • Activist catalyst: stocks where an activist could force a spinoff, sale, or restructuring to unlock value
  • Regulatory / legal catalysts: upcoming court cases or policy decisions that will materially reprice the stock
  • Balance sheet transformation: companies paying down debt aggressively — will re-rate once leverage drops below key thresholds
  • Accounting normalisation: one-time write-downs or unusual items temporarily depressing reported earnings
  • Catalyst timeline: each idea must have a specific catalyst with 6–18 month resolution — no 'cheap forever' open-ended theses

📖 Strategy details▼

Insider buying and 13F tracker — the only people with BOTH material information AND capital at stake. Cluster insider purchases and smart-money positioning are among the highest-conviction signals available.

  • Cluster buying: 3+ different insiders (CEO, CFO, board members) buying simultaneously within 30 days

  • Concentration signal: when a top fund puts 5%+ of their portfolio into one stock, that's conviction worth following
  • Combined signal: stocks showing BOTH insider buying AND smart-money accumulation — the highest-conviction setup
  • Red flags: cluster insider SELLING is equally informative — flag heavily selling insiders in holdings

🚀 Wood Disruptive Innovation — 3 picks ▼
🚀 Wood Disruptive Innovation
ARK Invest's disruptive-innovation screen. Pure-play names in AI, robotics, genomics, energy storage, or blockchain riding Wright's Law exponential cost curves.
📖 Strategy details▼

ARK Invest's disruptive innovation framework — identifying companies at the centre of major technological platforms that could produce exponential returns over the next decade via Wright's Law cost curves.

  • Five platforms: AI, robotics, energy storage, blockchain, and multiomics (genomics + proteomics)
  • Wright's Law: every doubling of production cuts costs predictably — ride the exponential adoption curve
  • Pure-plays only: 80%+ of revenue from the disruptive technology (not legacy businesses with a small AI division)
  • Convergence opportunities: companies at the intersection of 2+ platforms (AI + healthcare, AI + robotics)
  • TAM expansion: the total addressable market is itself growing as the technology matures
  • Network effects / data moats: companies that get stronger as they scale — more users, more data, better products
  • 5-year scenario: bull case (technology adopted as projected), base case, bear case (technology stalls)

🔢 Greenblatt Magic Formula — 2 picks ▼
🔢 Greenblatt Magic Formula
Joel Greenblatt's two-factor screen: ROIC >20% AND earnings yield >10%. Pre-filtered candidate pool; agent secondarily filters for sustainability and earnings quality.
📖 Strategy details▼

Joel Greenblatt's Magic Formula — the two-factor screen generating 30%+ annual returns over 20 years by finding companies that are simultaneously high-quality AND cheap.

  • ROIC above 20%: high-quality business generating strong returns on every dollar invested
  • Earnings yield above 10%: cheap relative to earnings (inverse of P/E) — the 'cheap' gate
  • Combined ranking: companies score best when BOTH metrics rank highly — quality + cheap together
  • Size filter: $50M–$10B to exclude illiquid micro-caps and already-covered mega-caps
  • Sector exclusions: skip financials, utilities, and REITs (different capital structures distort the formula)
  • Debt check: high ROIC shouldn't be mere financial leverage — verify balance sheet is clean
  • Earnings quality: free cash flow should match net income — flag accounting-driven earnings
  • Temporary vs permanent: is the stock cheap due to a fixable problem, or is the business structurally declining?

5Y growth: Equity: +2%/y

"THG's earnings trajectory tells a classic insurance recovery story that the market has barely noticed — operating income collapsed to just $41M in 2023 as catastrophe losses hammered the industry, then snapped back to $538M in 2024 and surged to $844M in 2025 as re-pricing took hold. Revenue has compounded cleanly from $5.4B to $6.6B. Yet the stock still trades at just 9.6x earnings and a PEG of 0.8, with a 30% ROIC and a near-pristine balance sheet (net debt of only $96M against $3.6B of equity). With only one analyst covering it, the market simply has not processed the magnitude of this earnings normalization. The 52-week price position at 99% of the high says institutional money is starting to wake up — but the valuation hasn't caught up. This is the kind of name Greenblatt loves: a quality compounder temporarily obscured by a cyclical trough, now emerging with pricing power baked in."

The market is still anchoring to 2022-2023 catastrophe-year earnings rather than the dramatically re-priced, structurally improved underwriting book now generating 30% ROIC.

Price $198 P/E 9.6 PEG 0.8 ROIC 30.2% MktCap $6.9B
